The snapshot is listed through `skynbd-api` and `skynbdctl` as any ref is. | Tests for: a cut counted before *C* with a higher group (the rewind case) is not published; a cut that does not open as itself is not published, and the publish goes to the first that does; an abandoned cut's publish goes to the next; the ref is swapped only after the commit it names is whole in object storage; a publish asked on one attachment is not carried out on the next. |
| **M2.6** `skyzfs-agent`: snapshot and publish | M2.5 | The agent takes `pool@t` with a channel program that records the manifest in the same transaction group (§5.4), reads the count of cuts before and `createtxg` after, places a hold on the snapshot, and asks the daemon to publish; the hold is released after the swap. A publish that does not complete in a stated time is helped along by a `zpool sync`: no cut comes on an idle pool, and the cut that would publish may have been recorded with a group that errs low or may not open as itself. A durable state record and a recovery planner as a pure function over what the record, the pool and object storage say, as in `skynbd-agent`. | The crash matrix at every step of publish, with the agent lost and with the daemon lost, on fakes of ZFS and of the daemon: after each, either `snap/t` is absent and a retry publishes it, or it names a cut that holds the snapshot. A snapshot destroyed before the hold is not published, and no hold is left behind after any loss. The agent publishes nothing for a pool that is not one plain data device on this volume with its log device (design §5.2, the premise): a mirror, a device being replaced or resilvered, a second data device. On a real pool it is M2.T5's. |
| **M2.7** `skyzfs-agent`: schedule, retention, a failed volume | M2.6 | A snapshot every minute by default; retention as ref deletion and `zfs destroy`; what design §10 gives the agent in M2: when the daemon reports the volume failed, the agent makes the pool read, which suspends it, and reports it lost for this boot; and after an import that leaves cuts that do not open as themselves (`zpool import -T`), the agent syncs the pool, a group at a time, until one does. | The schedule and the retention are tested on the fakes. The failed volume is shown on the daemon rig: the pool is suspended within seconds of the failure and the agent's report names the volume. M2.T4's round with a snapshot every minute runs on this agent. |
| **M2.T0b** The benchmark's times on status version 11 (skyzfs/skyzfs#93, merged 2026-11-14) | none; merges before M2.1 | The review of M2.1 found CI's `lint` red on it: `tests/bench/test_skz_time.py` reads the list of `time_*` keys from the daemon's source, which version 11 removes with the log store's times, and `skz_time.py` reads the pump's times as `time_pump_*`, which version 11 prints as `pump_*`, so the benchmark's time section would say "NO TIMES" on the new daemon. Both files take the daemon before and after: a row can still name an older build (`TB_SKYZFSD_REF`). | `lint` is green on `zfs-topology` with it and on M2.1's head once that has merged it in; a benchmark row on M2.1's head shows the pump's times. |
| **M2.T0** The rigs without the log store's status keys | M2.1's branch | M2.1's status version drops the keys that only the log store filled (`recovered_unacknowledged`, `unstored`, `checkpoint_step`, `log_live`, `checkpoint_steps`). Testing read its scripts on 2026-11-14 and found that they take a status without them since M1.3t: the benchmark's at-rest check counts an absent key as at rest, the replay rig takes an absent `checkpoint_steps` as none, and the daemon rig's two lines that read `recovered_unacknowledged` are NA on the daemon before the key is read. What is left is one observation's wording and the showing. It does not have to merge before M2.1. | On M2.1's head, with the tables as they are today: the daemon rig and the replay rig through the daemon (both run on that pull request by themselves), and one `zfs-skyzfsd` row of the benchmark between model rows, by dispatch. |
| **M2.T1** A reference for cuts, and recorded pools (skyzfs/skyzfs#94, merged 2026-11-15 at a8349da) | none | `tests/kernel/cut_ref.py`: from a record of the requests a server was handed, in the order received, the uberblock writes (parsed from the on-disk format, written without the daemon's parser), the cuts by §5.2 and the volume at each cut's instant. A workflow records real pools on Ubuntu 24.04 and 26.04, amd64 and arm64: create, load with snapshots, export and import, a rewind import, `multihost=on`, a change of the pool's configuration, stray flushes, `autotrim=on`, a nearly full pool, and the product's layout (design §6.1): a pool with a log device on a second device, under synchronous writes, each cut checked on the data device alone as a restore sees it (imported with the log device missing). Small records are kept as fixtures for M2.1. The daemon's request log lacks two things the reference needs, a write's content and the moment the store applied it; they come with M2.1 and M2.2. Until then the package records with dm-log-writes between ZFS and a loop device, in completion order and with every write's data, and the reference reads the replay rig's index format, so the daemon's log is fed to it later through a provider (M2.T3). | At every reference cut of every recorded pool, the volume replayed to that instant imports at the cut's group with every block the pool reaches verified in every copy (the replay rig's reader). That is design §5.2's claim itself, tested on real ZFS before the daemon builds on it. The reference's cuts also match, in order, the transaction groups ZFS says it synced (the pool's `txgs` kstat, read while recording): an import cannot tell a cut counted twice from one counted once, since both volumes import at the group. Negative controls with a table of what is expected, as the replay rig has: the instant one flush early, a block of the cut withheld, a block from after the instant; and two wrong rules for a repeat, each caught by the order check (testing's reading of the rewind, 2026-11-14): the group alone taken to identify a cut, which after a rewind import misses the new pool state written under a number already used, and every uberblock write counted, repeats included. |
| **M2.T2** Object storage and node B on the runners (skyzfs/skyzfs#97, merged 2026-11-15 at 83d41e4) | none; M2.T1 for its controls | An object store on the runner (the end-to-end tests already run MinIO), and node B: a second place on the runner that knows only the object store. It lists the refs, materialises one (through `skynbd-nbd`'s read-only export, or a file written from the engine), imports the pool read-only without its log device, compares snapshot *t* with what the source held (the checksums of its files), and verifies every block the pool reaches (`zdb -cc`, or a scrub of a throwaway copy). Built first on a stand-in for the uploader: a small tool that puts a reference cut of M2.T1 into object storage through the engine. | The stand-in's cut passes on node B, and each control is caught: a cut published one transaction group early (clean, without the snapshot), a cut that lacks a block, a cut with a later block, a hole where a live block was. The exit criterion's own words say why the first matters. |
| **M2.T3** The cut rig, on the daemon (stage 1: skyzfs/skyzfs#99; stage 2a, the content of held cuts: skyzfs/skyzfs#103, merged 2026-11-15 at 84f9175; 2b a take-over and 2c a failed volume to come) | M2.T1, M2.T2, and each coding package as it merges | The daemon's cuts held against the reference, stage by stage. After M2.1: the count and the groups. After M2.2: the content of held cuts (through the test-hooks command), with `kill -9` while a cut is held and with the bound reached. After M2.3: a power cut in the middle of an interval (the cut below the daemon that the replay rig makes, and a start as after a reboot). After M2.4: the same through object storage and node B, and a cut abandoned because its flush failed (check g's failing disk). After M2.5: a rewind import with cuts of the abandoned history held, then a publish, with the pool's groups still below the abandoned ones: node B imports what was published with no rewind option and finds the snapshot. | Each stage has its lines in a table of what is expected and fails in either direction. The stages after M2.4 are the exit criterion's four losses, each ending in node B's verification. |
| **M2.T4** Measurements | M2.2 for the first round | What M2 owes in figures: the size of the copies (by workload, by how far the upload lags, on a nearly full pool where ZFS reuses blocks at once), the cost of the cut state and of the intent map's sync on the write path (the benchmark's daemon rows against M1's daemon and the model, by M1's rules for a verdict), and the exit criterion: a snapshot every minute against none, p99.9, with the rule for "no more than run-to-run noise" written down before the round is run. | The figures go to the lead in full. A daemon with cuts on that leaves M1's rule against the model is a finding for the lead when it is seen, not at the end. |
| **M2.T5** The crash matrix of publish, on a real pool | M2.6 | The real agent and the real daemon lost at every step of a publish (hooks in the test-hooks builds), with node B's verification after each. | After each loss: `snap/t` absent and a retry publishes it, or `snap/t` names a cut that holds the snapshot and passes node B. |

### Which package shows which exit criterion

| Exit criterion (design §9) | Shown by |
|---|---|
| A snapshot on node A is listed on node B once its ref is swapped, imports read-only with snapshot *t* holding the source's content, and every block the pool reaches is verified | M2.T2's node B, on the daemon and the agent in M2.T3 and M2.T5 |
| The same for a cut after `kill -9` with a cut pending, after a power cut in the middle of an interval, and after an abandoned cut | M2.T3's stages after M2.4; under them the property tests of M2.2 and M2.3 and the crash matrix of M2.4 |
| A snapshot published after a rewind import, with cuts of the abandoned history held, is published by a cut that contains it | M2.T3's last stage; under it M2.1's repeat rule, M2.1b's cut that opens as itself and M2.5's count |
| A snapshot every minute changes p99.9 by no more than run-to-run noise | M2.T4, on M2.7's agent |
| The crash matrix passes at every step of publish | M2.6 on fakes, M2.T5 on a real pool |

### M2.1 as built (skyzfs/skyzfs#92)

The package's report is the pull request's description. What a reader of the design needs from it:

- **The parser** is the crate `skyzfs-uberblock`, with no I/O, citing OpenZFS 2.4.1 by file and line. The slot size is not known from a write, so every whole KiB of a ring that a write covers is read for the magic, in either byte order.
- **The rule in the store** is `crates/skyzfs-local/src/inplace/cuts.rs`. A write that reaches a ring is parsed when it is received; its uberblocks mark once the direct write has returned whole and the store has taken the result, before the completion goes to the daemon; the first flush received after a mark carries the cut, and the cut is counted where the store executes that flush, which it reaches only with no write busy and nothing waiting ahead.
- **A repeat is also compared with the cut a flush is carrying to its barrier** (Codex's finding, confirmed by the review): a label write applied between a stray flush's receipt and its barrier would otherwise cut one state twice. The design's rule now names it.
- **Eight mutants, each failed by a test**, the untested half of the flush barrier among them (`PASSES_WAITING_FLUSH`). Two more tests hold the barrier's place for a flush that waits behind a sync under way and for one that joins it.
- **Status version 11** (`SKYZST11`) adds `cuts` and `cut_group` and drops every field that only the log store filled, and every `time_*` key; the pump's times are `pump_*`. Versions 8 to 10 are still read.
- **The request log** of the test-hooks build gained `label` (a label write's bytes), `applied`, `barrier` and `cut`.
- **On the rigs, on the merged head c1dc842:** the daemon rig's table was as expected on Ubuntu 24.04, 26.04 and 26.04-arm (run 43642725554: 56 lines PASS on each, the judge's exit 0 on the artifacts), the replay rig's daemon jobs and the write-into-a-hole jobs passed (43642725624), and the control rig (43642725607). One benchmark row on b89d82b between two model rows, one runner: 3843 tps against 3809 and 3818, ratio 1.008 (run 43636338975). A second row read with M2.T0b's reader showed the pump's times and 24 cuts in a 120 s window (43640755028); in it the pump's `taken` counters read 0 for the daemon's whole life, which is put to coding.
- **Not in it:** the test against recorded pools (with M2.1b), the checksum (M2.1c), any state that outlives the daemon (M2.2): the count starts at 0 at each start.

### M2.2 as built (skyzfs/skyzfs#98, #100, #101)

**The state, and what it locks.** Header, maps and records are one region, `<run dir>/<volume dir>/cuts.state` on tmpfs, allocated in full, mapped shared and locked (crate `skyzfs-mapped`). For a TiB of volume at 4 KiB blocks it is 148 MiB since M2.4e (146 MiB before it: the hole set adds a bit for each 64 KiB, 2 MiB per TiB; a test pins the figure):

| Part | Size | For a TiB at 4 KiB |
|---|---|---|
| Four maps of one bit a block: the cut being uploaded, the cut that waits, the cut whose flush is under way, the open map | 4 bits a block | 128 MiB |
| A summary bit for each 64-bit word of each map | 1/64 of the maps | 2 MiB |
| Records of copies, 16 bytes each, one for each block of the bound | bound / 256 | 16 MiB at the provisional bound (volume/256, 4 GiB) |
| Header: the count, the marks, three held cuts, the newest cut's state, the count of abandoned cuts, the owner (boot, volume identity, start token) | about 1 KiB | |

The copies themselves are in `copies.img` beside the image, at most the bound, on disk, not locked and not allocated ahead, written and read by direct I/O since M2.2c. A freeze is one store into the region; taking a map is three stores; every change is ordered so that a death after any store leaves a state the next start takes up and finishes. Format version 1.

**Trust.** `volume.start` in the volume's directory is the start token (design §5.2's start generation): sixteen random bytes, written and synced by every open before the marker goes and before anything is served, with or without `--hold-cuts`, and by a start that is then refused (the daemon rig's pin was widened for it, skyzfs/skyzfs#102). A start that finds the volume held by another instance writes nothing. A state is taken up only if it names this boot, this volume and the token its open found, and no sync failed; otherwise its held cuts are let go and the next cut counts the whole volume. The cost: a start lost between its open and its take-up leaves the held cuts behind. A state file of another length is emptied before it is given the length asked.

**The maps are behind an interface** (`state/maps.rs`: contains, insert, move one into another, clear, the cost of a pass, list), for goal 9. The flat layout above is what M2 builds. A layout in proportion to a cache, for M3, would be a pool of chunks sized by the cache and a directory for each map: `insert` names a clean chunk in one store before the bit, a move renames a directory entry or ORs the chunk. The protocol does not change; what changes is the pool running out. A chunk counted as changed whole keeps a held map a superset, which is safe for an upload, but the open map must stay exact (a block in it needs no copy), and a record's `lo` must come from a map that holds the block exactly. So the open map gets chunks first and a held map gives them up by counting them whole. That reworks the copy rule's argument and is M3's.

**The rigs' interface (test-hooks build).** `kill -USR2` makes the daemon read one line from `cut-command` in the volume's run directory and answer in `cut-reply` (written beside and renamed), each tagged with a token of the rig's: `held` lists the held cuts (number, group, phase, whether it opens as itself, blocks); `write N PATH` writes every block of cut N's map into PATH as the cut reads it, 1 MiB after each turn of the queue, and lists the blocks in `PATH.blocks`; `release N` lets the cut being uploaded go. A cut whose flush is under way is refused. After the volume has failed the commands go on, as design §5.2 has the instance that saw the failure still upload a cut whose flush had completed (a first version refused them, on a remark the review took back). Serve, however, ends with status 3 within about two seconds of a failure, so no held cut is written after it in practice; the lead decided on 2026-11-17 that stage 2c pins that (a volume that fails with a cut held ends with 3, the next start is refused without `--acknowledge-failure`, a start with it begins a new state, and its first cut is whole) and that uploading the cut after a failure is judged in M2.5. `SIGUSR2` is ignored by a default build. Status version 13 adds the state (`off`, `taken_up`, `begun`), the held cuts, the bytes copied, the bound and the abandoned count; the request log can carry each write's content, whole or as a hash for each block.

**Found on the way.** The review's first look at M2.2b-1 found two ways a stale state was trusted (a start that held no cuts between two that did; an acknowledged failure lost before the new state began), which the start token closes for every instance the state never saw. Open after it: a copy can be lost with no trace while copies are buffered (M2.2c).

### The content of held cuts, first run (M2.T3 stage 2a, skyzfs/skyzfs#103, 2026-11-15)

One run on one Ubuntu 26.04 runner (43701571141), the daemon of `zfs-topology` at 1df9b32 with `--hold-cuts`. Beside each recording's workload a holder loops: list the held cuts, write the one being uploaded onto an image, hash every block, release. Every cut written holds the reference's content at its instant, block by block, rebuilt from the request log's hashes. Cuts written of cuts counted: life 5 of 26, slog 6 of 36, rewind 19 of 32, rewindsync 13 of 32, rewindf 10 of 29, multihost 34 of 87, config 6 of 26, stray 2 of 18, autotrim 7 of 40 (381688 comparisons of discarded blocks, where the daemon kept the old content), full 56 of 218. The cuts not written were replaced while they waited. Both content controls are caught on every recording. No cut was abandoned at the bound and no write-zeroes appeared in the ten, so two recordings were added (run 43702505054, one run each on Ubuntu 26.04 and 26.04-arm, all twelve recordings pass). `bound`: the holder keeps the first cut while the pool writes 320 MiB; one cut is abandoned at the 256 MiB bound on both images, and the cuts written after it (3 and 2) hold the reference's content. `edges`: a write-zeroes and a discard sent straight to the device; the last written cut holds the zeroed range as zeros. A request that covers a block in part cannot be driven, since the device's logical block is 4 KiB: that path rests on unit tests.

With M2.2c merged in (run 43713302430, one run on each of the two images), the kernel drove direct copies with cuts held for the first time: all twelve recordings pass on both, one cut abandoned at the bound on each, and every recording but one read blocks from copies in a written cut (for example `full`: 1621 blocks in the first written cut and 122 in later ones on 26.04, 1873 and 57 on arm; `bound`: about 21,000 in the first). The holders wrote more cuts than on the head before (full 55 against 39), on other runners, which shows nothing about speed.

### M2.3 as built (skyzfs/skyzfs#105, #106)

**The file.** `intent.map` in the volume's directory, on the image's file system, written by direct I/O. Page 0 is the header: format version, the volume's identity, the region size, the start token of the start that keeps the map, the base (number and group of the newest committed cut, or none) and a checksum. Each further 4 KiB page holds bits for 32,672 regions under its own checksum. A daemon keeps the map when it holds cuts (`--hold-cuts`), with `--intent-region-mib` for the region.

**The write path.** A write or write-zeroes into a region whose bit is not durable sets the bit in memory and waits, as a request waits behind an overlapping one; requests that overlap it and flushes wait behind it, others do not. The file's helper thread syncs the map once for every bit set since its last sync; the page writes themselves (one for each bit page with a new bit) are made on the queue's thread. A write into a region already named does not wait. A failed write or sync of the map fails the volume. Discards are not noted, as in the cut state.

**Clearing.** When a cut is committed (M2.4; until then the test-hooks `release`), the header with the new base is written, then the bits are cleared of every region with no block in a map newer than the cut, both without a sync. A power cut that loses the header leaves the base of the cut before: the uploader's check then finds a mismatch and the next cut is whole. A clear that a power cut loses names a region too many. Whether to sync the header at each release is decided with M2.4c's figures (Q7, lead, 2026-11-17).

**The start.** The map is taken up only if its format, identity and region size match, it names the token of the start before this one, every page's checksum holds and it has a base. A start that acknowledges a failure takes it up too: to the map a failed volume is a power cut, since no write begins before its bit is durable. A map taken up narrows a new cut state's "whole volume" to the blocks of the regions it names; the named regions are counted as changed whole, with no comparison against the uploaded cut. A new volume's map names every region. The start records its token in the header before it serves, and syncs the directory when it creates the file. A start refused for a file system without direct I/O neither creates nor writes the map. A cut state file that survives a power cut stale is not taken up while the map is (the store-level power-cut test, with four mutants).

**Status version 14** adds `intent_state` (`off`, `taken_up`, `begun`, `unsaid`), the region size, the regions, how many are named now and were named at the start, the syncs on the write path with their summed and largest time, and the writes that waited.

**Found on the way.** Two writes of a map page during one sync left a bit called durable (review and Codex on M2.3a). The start that created the file did not sync its directory, and the store's drop hung while a write waited for the map (review and Codex on M2.3b). All fixed before the merges.

### What the first recordings of real pools showed (M2.T1, 2026-11-14)

One runner, one pass (Ubuntu 26.04, kernel 7.0.0-1019-azure, ZFS 2.4.1; run 43631961753), before the package's pull request: a 512 MiB pool at ashift 12 on dm-log-writes, recorded from `zpool create`. Each reference cut was checked three ways: `zdb` opens the volume at the cut's group, every copy of every reachable block passes its checksum, and the kernel imports it read-only.

| Recording | Cuts that passed all three |
|---|---|
| life | 27 of 27 |
| a change of configuration (comment, reguid, a mirror attached and detached) | 26 of 26 |
| stray flushes (an fsync of the device every 2 ms) | 17 of 17 |
| `autotrim=on` | 41 of 41 |
| a full pool (filled to ENOSPC, rewritten) | 218 of 218 |
| a rewind (`zpool import -T 37` from group 51) | 26 of 32 |

- **The design's claim held at 329 of 329 cuts outside the rewind**: the volume at a cut's instant is a complete pool at the cut's group. On one runner; the four images run on the pull request.
- **After `import -T`, six cuts did not open as themselves.** The new history's cuts at groups 39 to 48 are whole at their groups, and `zdb -e -u` on each selects group 51 of the abandoned history, still in the rings. The first that opened as itself was the new history's 51. That is now a rule of the design (§5.2, "Whether a cut opens as itself"), a package (M2.1b) and a row of §10. Whether the abandoned state is still whole at those instants is being recorded.
- **ZFS 2.4 commits groups that write nothing and writes no uberblock for them.** The `txgs` kstat lists them with `nwritten` 0 (19 committed and 11 that wrote in one session, 99 and 37 with autotrim, 260 and 214 on the full pool). Against the committed groups that wrote, the reference's cuts were equal, in order, in all eight sessions compared. The order check compares with those.
- **No write carried FUA**, on an uberblock or elsewhere. No flush, stray or not, fell between two copies of an uberblock, so the rule that counts repeats made the same cuts everywhere; the rule that takes the group alone was caught on the rewind.
- **The controls** "one flush early" and "a block withheld" were caught in all six recordings; "a later block" in the two long ones (the short ones had no later write over a block the cut reaches, and the control now tries earlier cuts).
- **Not judged yet** in that run: `multihost=on` (the rig's check imported under another host id and was refused), whether a writable import completes a cut before it returns, and `zpool import -F`.

A second run the same day (43637989615, the same kind of runner, one pass, five recordings before a bug of the rig stopped it) answered three of the questions the plan had put to the recordings:

- **A writable create or import completes cuts before it returns**: two or three, each an uberblock and its flush, in all 8 sessions of the 5 recordings (the import after `-T 37` made groups 39 to 41 before `zpool import` returned). The design's argument that a cut's group cannot err high for a publish rests on this.
- **The rewind, again, and what the abandoned state is worth.** The same six cuts did not open as themselves. The abandoned group 51 that the volume opens at was whole at each of those instants (every copy of 561 blocks, 781 copies; a read-only import), and each cut was whole at its own group when opened with a rewind to it (563 to 629 blocks). The reference now reads every uberblock slot through the record and predicts which one ZFS picks at each cut; `zdb` picked exactly that one at all 32 cuts. One recording shows that the abandoned state can be whole in the window, not that it always is.
- **Syncing past the abandoned groups closes the window in well under a second.** Rewound to 37 with the abandoned newest at 51: four syncs, each with a 4 KiB write, took 73 ms until group 52 wrote. The cuts at groups 39 to 49 opened at 51; the cut at 52 and the four after it opened as themselves.
- **The product's layout** (the data device recorded, a 256 MiB log device beside it, 76 `O_SYNC` writes of 4 KiB during the load): cuts 5 to 28 passed every check on the data device alone, which are `zdb`'s choice of the cut's group, every copy of every reachable block, and `zpool import -m -o readonly=on`. The import comes up DEGRADED and calls the log device a missing device (`logs: <guid> UNAVAIL`, ZFS-8000-4J); it says nothing of intent-log records. `zdb` opened the data device alone. Cuts 1 to 4, the create's own groups, were not judged in that run (the rig gave `zdb` a copy of the log device from after the export).

A third run (43638676548, one runner, one pass; the run itself failed on two errors of the rig, in the multihost and `-F` recordings):

- **The abandoned state inside the rewind window can be damaged.** In the recording with syncs after `import -T` (rewound to 37, abandoned up to 51, four syncs in 710 ms until group 52), the five cuts below 51 open at the abandoned 51, and that state is not whole there: 13 of 783 copies fail at the first of them and 16 at the other four, the new history having written over blocks of the abandoned one (the first is copy 2 of 3 of its MOS objset block). Whether any block lost every copy is being recorded. Each of those cuts is whole at its own group, opened with a rewind to it. In the recording without syncs the abandoned state was whole at all six such cuts, again. So a cut that does not open as itself can open at a damaged pool, and its restore needs the rewind to its group: the reason it is recorded so and never published (design §5.2, §10).
- **Every other recording passed at every cut**: life 27, the log-device layout 29 (the create's four groups now judged), rewind 32 and rewind with syncs 33 (each at its own group), configuration 26, stray flushes 17, autotrim 40, the full pool 219. A create or an import completed cuts before it returned in every session. The controls were as expected, and the rule that counts repeats was caught for the first time, in the stray recording with the back labels' writes delayed (27 cuts against 17).
- **A multihost pool that was not exported cannot be imported read-only** (corrected by the fourth run, below: not from a writable copy either): ZFS 2.4's activity check claims the pool by writing and reading back uberblocks even with `readonly=on` (`spa_activity_check_claim`), and 88 of the rig's 92 read-only imports were refused. The rig now imports such a cut from a writable copy. A restore of a multihost pool's cut on another node meets the same thing (M3). One multihost cut was a second cut of a state already cut.
- **`zpool import -F` refused** a pool whose newest group's root block was zeroed in all three copies ("one or more devices is currently unavailable"); the recording now keeps what the import says and tries `-FX`.

A fourth run (43645075993, one runner, one pass) corrected one of those points and added one:

- **A multihost pool that was not exported cannot be imported read-only at all** on ZFS 2.4.1, from a writable device either: the claim writes only to leaves opened for writing, and `readonly=on` opens none, so it gets no good write and the import is refused as "imported on host '<unknown>' (hostid=0)". Such a pool's cut can only be imported writable, after the activity wait and the claim's writes. Node B (M2.T2) and a restore (M3) work on a copy or a clone for that reason; `tests/kernel/cut-record.sh` says so in its header. In this run all 95 multihost cuts opened as themselves and none was a second cut of a state.
- **After an export, `zpool import -F` cannot rewind, and `-FX` can.** With the three copies of the newest group's root block zeroed, `-F` was refused: the export's last groups had written nothing, so the rewind found an uberblock older than the labels' configuration, which the export had rewritten ("label discarded as txg is too large (43 > 34)"). `-FX` imported, and during the import wrote the failed group's number again with another root block pointer: the new pool state under a used number that the repeat rule is for, here on the documented recovery path. All 29 cuts of that recording were whole, and the rule that takes the group alone was caught on it once the check also looked for a state the rule drops.
- **The damage of the abandoned state did not recur**: in the same recording with syncs, the abandoned 51 was whole at all five cuts this time (559 blocks, 776 copies). Whether it is written over depends on where the new history allocates; the third run stands as one occurrence, with no answer yet on whether a block lost every copy.
- Every other recording passed at every cut again (life 27, the log-device layout 29, rewind 31, rewind with syncs 32, `-FX` 29, configuration, stray flushes, autotrim, the full pool).

On the pull request's last head (a8349da, cut rig run 43659159452, one runner for each of the four images, ten recordings each, every check's exit 0): 2166 cuts, all whole at their groups (Ubuntu 26.04: 537, 26.04-arm: 541, 24.04: 544, 24.04-arm: 544), every control as expected, `-FX` importing on every image, and one multihost cut that was a second cut of a state (24.04-arm). The fixtures in `tests/fixtures/pools` are from run 43647779663. The damaged abandoned state after `import -T` has been seen in 4 of 15 run-images: once on 26.04 and in each of the three runs on 24.04-arm (28 to 31 of 791 copies in the last, in 15 to 16 blocks), and in those three no block had lost every copy. Why that image is not known. The judge compares `zdb`'s root block pointer with the cut's, and not only the group and the timestamp (Codex's finding on the pull request).

**Node B and the log device (M2.T2, review of skyzfs/skyzfs#97, 2026-11-15).** `zdb -u` opens a cut of the data device alone, but `zdb`'s walk of every block (`-c`, `-cc`) does not when the pool's intent log is on a log device node B does not have: the walk reads the intent log's first block, the read fails with ENXIO, and zdb stops (zio.c 5705, zdb.c 10117, dmu_traverse.c 144 in 2.4.1, as the review reads them). The lead's ruling: the rig's check, not node B, puts a stand-in for the log device beside the image for zdb, as M2.T1's judge does; the image judged is the one read from the bucket, and the kernel's import with `-m` and no log device stays in the check as what a restore does. A scrub of a writable throwaway copy was the other way and is not taken: its first group replaces the newest metadata, so a bad spare copy of a replaced block would go unread. Design §9's criterion says so now.

**Node B as built (M2.T2, skyzfs/skyzfs#97).** `bins/skyzfs-nodeb` is both the stand-in for the uploader (an image through the engine, and the ref `snap/t`) and node B, which has only the bucket (refs through `ObjectRefStore`, no lease; the refs are in the bucket only with `refs_kind = "object"`, and with redb refs node B would need `rebuild_refs`). `tests/kernel/nodeb_check.py` judges, and `skyzfs-nodeb-rig.yml` runs MinIO and the `life` and `slog` recordings on both 26.04 images on a pull request. On the merged head (run 43671750087, one runner an image): both recordings pass every stage, `slog` imported with `-m`; and each control is caught at its stage: the source's digest changed, at the files; a cut one group early, at the snapshot, with the import and every copy passing, which is the case the exit criterion names; a cut that lacks a block, at the copies; a later block, at the import (an uberblock of a later group: no write after the instant touched a block the cut reaches); a hole for a file's block, at the files. Not done: a multihost pool ends as the rig's error, and the intent log is not judged.

**The daemon's cuts against the reference (M2.T3, first stage, skyzfs/skyzfs#99).** `skyzfs-cut-daemon.yml` records the ten pools through `skyzfsd` and holds the daemon's own cut lines, from its request log, against the reference: the count, the flush each cut falls on, its group, and whether it opens as itself. In the first run on the merged daemon with M2.1c (dispatch 43675946002, one Ubuntu 26.04 runner) they were equal in all ten recordings: life 26 cuts, the log-device layout 36, rewind 32 (26 that open as themselves), rewind with syncs 32 (27), `-FX` 29, multihost 87, configuration 26, stray flushes 18, autotrim 40, the full pool 219; the status, the sessions and the import's syncs passed and the four controls were caught. On the pull request's last head (073873b, run 43681839616) all ten passed on 26.04 and 26.04-arm. One failure on the way was the check's and not the daemon's: a stray flush received before a group's uberblock writes were applied, with its barrier after them, marks no cut by design §5.2, as the daemon had it; the reference had placed a flush only at its barrier, and now takes a flush as a receipt and a barrier.

### The intent map's region size, first figures (M2.3, 2026-11-15)

Coding replayed M2.T1's nine recorded pools (512 MiB each, 13,946 writes in all) through the intent map's rules. "Named" is the bytes the map names at a flush; "changed" is the 4 KiB blocks written since the newest committed cut, where the commit comes one cut after the cut (lag 1) or four (lag 4). Medians over the pools; the mean changed at lag 1 was 2.54 MiB.

| Region | Writes that sync the map, lag 1 | Named / changed, lag 1 (range) | Mean named, lag 1 | Writes that sync, lag 4 | Named / changed, lag 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 256 KiB | 15.6% | 1.6 (1.5 to 2.2) | 4.2 MiB | 13.8% | 1.2 |
| 1 MiB | 4.4% | 2.9 (2.5 to 4.4) | 7.3 MiB | 3.6% | 1.6 |
| 4 MiB | 1.7% | 8.9 (7.0 to 15.2) | 22.3 MiB | 1.2% | 3.5 |
| 16 MiB | 1.0% | 31.8 (23.6 to 53.8) | 78.9 MiB | 0.6% | 10.7 |
| 64 MiB | 0.4% | 88 (63 to 170) | 224.9 MiB | 0.2% | 26.6 |

At 64 MiB the map names about 44% of these small pools at every flush; 1 to 4 MiB names a few times what changed, where counting the named regions whole is good enough and a comparison with the uploaded cut would buy little. The pools are small and their workloads short, so the shares are not a large volume's. The default was set to 4 MiB from these and M2.T4's second round (below).

### The write path with held cuts, round 1 (M2.T4, 2026-11-15)

Run 43720102842: one sequence on one machine (Ubuntu 26.04, x86_64), no origin delay, scale 100, 8 clients, 120 s a workload, release builds. One run behind every figure, and the model rows moved during it (tpcb from 2765 to 3964 tps, select from 26572 to 17259), so most rows have no verdict.

**What the rows are.** No cut is ever released in a release build, so the copies run up to the 256 MiB bound, the cut is abandoned and it starts again (7 to 20 cuts abandoned of about 24 in a window): every first write of a block after a cut copies. That is the copy path's worst case, not an upload's steady state. The intent map does no work in any row: a new volume's state names every region at its start and nothing clears a bit, so `intent_syncs` and `intent_waits` are 0, and the three map rows differ from "no map" only in the build.

| Row | tpcb tps | p50 / p99 / p99.9 / max, ms | Abandoned | select tps (p99 ms) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| model 1 | 2765 | 1.50 / 30.0 / 172 / 474 | | 26572 (1.01) |
| map 64 MiB, direct copies (M2.3b at e9281c9) | 2328 | 1.69 / 27.6 / 175 / 495 | 10 | 15854 (4.5) |
| map 4 MiB, direct copies | 1546 | 1.71 / 88.8 / 237 / 401 | 7 | 17035 (3.7) |
| model 2 | 3694 | 1.51 / 11.0 / 69 / 1169 | | 25713 (1.08) |
| map 1 MiB, direct copies | 1938 | 1.85 / 47.8 / 228 / 519 | 9 | 16479 (3.7) |
| no map, direct copies (0c3df3c) | 1460 | 1.69 / 102.0 / 315 / 541 | 7 | 17252 (4.3) |
| model 3 | 3964 | 1.49 / 9.9 / 71 / 494 | | 17259 (1.26) |
| buffered copies (9e48d3f, before M2.2c) | 3006 | 1.62 / 25.2 / 117 / 547 | 14 | 22024 (1.32) |
| no held cuts (M2.3b without `--hold-cuts`) | 3016 | 1.58 / 23.0 / 149 / 811 | 0 | 19594 (1.29) |
| model 4 | 3529 | 1.50 / 13.5 / 83 / 547 | | 19373 (1.17) |

Ratios to the enclosing model rows on tpcb: map 1 MiB 0.506 and no map 0.381 (both below 0.90, bracket 7.0%); map 64 MiB 0.721, map 4 MiB 0.479, buffered 0.802, no held cuts 0.805 (no verdict, brackets 11.6% to 28.8%).

**Reading.** In this regime direct copies cost about half the throughput and buffered copies cost nothing that one run can see: the four rows with direct copies run at 1460 to 2328 tps with p99 of 28 to 102 ms, while buffered copies and no held cuts sit together at about 3010 tps and p99 of 23 to 25 ms. The work disk takes 2.6 to 2.8 writes a transaction with direct copies against 0.8 with buffered ones: a direct read and a direct write for each copied block, made on the queue's thread, as the review of M2.2c foresaw (its R3). The spread among the direct rows is as large as the models' drift, so the run ranks no region size. Round 2 (test-hooks builds with a holder that releases every cut, and every fourth) shows whether the cost holds when cuts come and go as under an upload.

### The intent map's cost with cuts released, round 2 (M2.T4, 2026-11-15)

Run 43726791081: one sequence on one machine (AWS, Ubuntu 26.04, x86_64, work disk write-through), no origin delay, scale 100, 8 clients, 120 s a workload; one run behind every figure. The machine was steady (tpcb models 7603 to 7858, brackets 0.4% to 3.3%). Every daemon row is a test-hooks build with `--hold-cuts`; a releaser beside the workload lets cuts go as an upload would, every cut at once (r1) or each once three newer ones are taken (r4). In the 120 s tpcb window that was 23 releases at r1, one for each transaction group, and 7 or 8 at r4; in the select window 24 or 25, and 8 (the review's count from the run's artifact; 98 and 31 or 32 over a daemon's whole life). The releaser polls the daemon with 8.5 commands a second, answered on the queue's thread, in every daemon row alike. No cut was abandoned and hardly any write had to be copied, so the rows show the map's cost and not the copies'.

| Row | tpcb tps | Ratio to model | Map syncs / writes that waited | Sync time, sum / largest | select tps (ratio) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| r1, 64 MiB | 6886 | 0.891 | 188 / 278 | 64 ms / 19 ms | 46690 (0.847) |
| r1, 4 MiB | 6629 | 0.857 | 1196 / 1573 | 222 ms / 18 ms | 46140 (0.837) |
| r1, 1 MiB | 6277 | 0.801 | 3858 / 4899 | 867 ms / 45 ms | 39967 (0.717) |
| r1, no map | 6790 | 0.866 | | | 47318 (0.849) |
| r4, 64 MiB | 6874 | 0.885 | 86 / 110 | 14 ms / 1 ms | 47179 (0.842) |
| r4, 4 MiB | 6754 | 0.869 | 1187 / 1738 | 236 ms / 18 ms | 45278 (0.808) |
| r4, 1 MiB | 6381 | 0.821 | 3800 / 5476 | 869 ms / 31 ms | 40564 (0.720) |
| r4, no map | 6982 | 0.899 | | | 45994 (0.817) |

p99 was 3.9 to 4.4 ms in every daemon row (models 3.5 to 3.8) and p99.9 8 ms throughout. Against the no-map build at the same release rate: 64 MiB costs nothing that one run shows; 4 MiB costs 2 to 3% on tpcb and about 2% on select, too little to separate from one run; 1 MiB costs 7.6 to 8.6% on tpcb and 12 to 16% on select, more than its summed sync time explains, so the queue's thread is held by the map's page writes too. The release rate barely matters. **Every daemon row is below 0.90 of the model, the no-map rows included**: the test-hooks build with held cuts is itself at 0.82 to 0.90 here, where M1's criterion had the release build without held cuts at the model's level. What part of that is the test-hooks build and what part the held cuts is not separated yet.

**The default region is 4 MiB** (lead, 2026-11-15): at 64 MiB the map names about 88 times what changed on the recorded pools, at 4 MiB about 9 times for a cost one run cannot separate from none, and 1 MiB costs too much. Nine 4 KiB pages of map for a TiB (a page covers 32,672 regions).

### What separates the held-cuts rows from the model, round 3 (M2.T4, 2026-11-17)

Run 43739522080: fifteen rows on one machine (Azure runnervmccnms, slow syncs: buffered sync 337 ms), one run per row, two of each of A to D, one of E. The daemon is 1cf30ff, so C's copies are single-block ones, from before #107. C, D and E serve with `--hold-cuts --intent-region-mib 4`; D and E release every cut. The tpcb models drifted early and were steady from the third on (5653, 5879, 6654, 6581, 6642, 6710).

| row | build, serve | tpcb tps | ratio (bracket) | p99 ms | select ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | release, no hold | 5907 / 6340 | 1.024 (3.9%) / 0.958 (1.1%) | 5.7 / 4.7 | 0.840 / 0.864 |
| B | +hooks, no hold | 5884 / 6158 | 1.020 (3.9%) / 0.931 (0.9%) | 4.7 / 5.5 | 0.869 / 0.838 |
| C | +hooks, hold, no releaser | 3904 / 4053 | 0.623 (no verdict) / 0.613 (0.9%) | 9.8 / 9.7 | 0.699 / 0.762 |
| D | +hooks, hold, released | 5672 / 6129 | 0.905 (no verdict) / 0.918 (1.0%) | 5.2 / 4.8 | 0.817 / 0.848 |
| E | D with perf | 6166 | no ratio | 4.8 | |

- The release build reaches the model on this machine (1.024, 0.958). On round 2's machine every row was 0.80 to 0.90; this sequence does not explain that gap, so it depends on the machine.
- The hooks cost 0.4% and 2.9% of tps (B against A). Held cuts with a releaser at 4 MiB cost under 0.5% more (D against B, second pair). The map's syncs: 953 to 1043 in the window, 377 to 427 ms in all, the largest 14 to 31 ms; 24 releases in the window in each D and E row.
- Held cuts without releases are the copy path at its worst on a slow-sync disk: 0.61 to 0.62, twelve cuts abandoned in each window, work-disk writes per transaction 2.17 to 2.19 against 0.66 to 0.69 for A, B and D.
- select: every daemon row but C is at 0.82 to 0.87, A included. That gap is the daemon's read path, not the hooks, the held cuts or the releaser.
- Profile of E (20 s at 999 Hz, tpcb): the queue thread had 2438 samples, on a CPU about 12% of the time. 50% in the io_uring wait and its syscalls; 11.9% under `skyzfsd::record::` (the test-hooks `Recorded` wrapper), of which 6.0% is `record::columns` formatting a request-log line with no `--request-log`; 2.8% `IntentMap::named_count` as a leaf; 2.7% a pipe read; 1.3% `cut_commands` (the releaser's polls); 0.4% `store_shown`. Stacks are short (no frame pointers), so these are lower bounds. Not separated: who calls `named_count` (status reader, `held` replies or the write path).

So the earlier 0.72 to 0.90 was the machine and, in part, the test-hooks build, which formats every request on the queue's thread (6 to 12% of that thread's CPU, 0.4 to 2.9% of tps). The release build with held cuts and a releaser has not been measured; the M2.4 daemon is that build, so the figure comes with M2.4c. Open: do not format a log line in the hooks build when no request log is given; find `named_count`'s caller.

### Copies in runs against copies by the block (M2.T4, M2.2d, 2026-11-15)

Two sequences on two machines, each compared only within itself; one run a row; every daemon row a test-hooks build with `--hold-cuts`. S1 is run 43731184255 (AWS, fast syncs, tpcb models 7742 to 8047), S2 run 43731234507 (Azure, models 3571 to 3624). "d" is M2.2d at b6aaad8 (copies in runs), "c" M2.2c (one 4 KiB block a copy), "b" the build before M2.2c (buffered copies); 0 is no release (round 1's regime: copies up to the 256 MiB bound, then the cut is abandoned, 24 or 25 times in S1's window and 10 or 11 in S2's), 1 releases each cut, 4 the uploading cut once three newer are taken. Ratios are to the mean of the enclosing model rows.

| Row | S1 tpcb tps | S1 ratio | S1 p99, ms | S2 tpcb tps | S2 ratio | S2 p99, ms | select ratio, S1 / S2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| c0 | 5854 | 0.750 | 10.1 | 2593 | 0.719 | 12.5 | 0.813 / 0.782 |
| d0 | 6431 | 0.824 | 7.2 | 3275 | 0.907 | 6.7 | 0.826 / 0.836 |
| b0 | 6790 | 0.870 | 4.7 | 3319 | 0.924 | 6.4 | 0.823 / 0.855 |
| d1 | 6830 | 0.875 | 4.0 | 3407 | 0.945 | 6.1 | 0.824 / 0.863 |
| c1 | 7067 | 0.899 | 3.9 | 3351 | 0.933 | 6.2 | 0.852 / 0.854 |
| d4 | 6951 | 0.885 | 3.9 | 3370 | 0.941 | 6.3 | 0.835 / 0.852 |
| c4 | 7096 | 0.893 | 3.8 | 3383 | 0.937 | 6.3 | 0.844 / 0.857 |
| no held cuts | | | | 3378 | 0.943 | 6.2 | / 0.860 |

Work-disk writes a transaction: S1 models 3.56 to 3.63, c0 5.15, d0 4.85, b0 3.94, rows with releases 3.60 to 3.75; S2 models 0.98 to 1.02, c0 2.60, d0 1.14, every other daemon row 0.87 to 0.88.

**Reading.** Without releases, copies in runs recover most of what copies by the block lost: d0 is 9.9% (S1) and 26% (S2) above c0, with p99 of about 7 ms against 10 to 12. It stays below buffered copies by 4.6 (S1) and 1.7 (S2) ratio points, and below itself with releases by 5.1 and 3.8. With releases no cut is abandoned, nothing stays copied and the copy path does not matter: d and c are within 0.3 to 3.5% of each other in both directions, and on S2 they sit with no held cuts at 0.93 to 0.95. The head measured is b6aaad8, before M2.2d's last fix.

**Decided (lead, 2026-11-15): the copy stays on the queue's thread for now.** M2.2e (the copy on the I/O thread that makes the write) is not built before M2.4. Its gain is bounded by the 2 to 5 points above, it is paid only while an upload lags far enough for writes to land on a held cut's blocks, and it reworks the copy rule's order under a freeze (the rules in M2.2d's row). It is judged again with the real uploader, where how often a write meets a held cut is measured and not staged.

### What is open in M2

- **The bound's default.** D7 gives the rule and no number. M2.2 is built with a provisional default so that the rigs run (below); the default is set from M2.T4's figures.
- **Compare or re-upload after a power cut.** The design says the intent map's regions are compared with the newest uploaded cut. Counting them as changed whole is a superset and needs no read from object storage; M2.3 measures both. Decided 2026-11-15: M2.3 counts the named regions whole (it has no engine to read the uploaded cut with); the first figures (above) say that is enough at regions of 1 to 4 MiB, and the comparison is M2.4's only if the default ends up large.
- **The store through M2 is `complete`** (design §5.1, "Where this stands"): the whole volume on local flash, a sparse image, no eviction and no fetch. The owner's goal 9 (2026-11-14), volumes larger than the local store behind a cache of a set size, is M3's `cache` policy and is planned first in M3 (the owner agreed to M3 for it on 2026-11-15). What it asks of M2 already: M2.2's state is locked memory in proportion to the volume (130 MiB a TiB), which a very large volume cannot have; coding is asked how the layout extends to state in proportion to the cache.
- **How the agent reaches the daemon.** M2.5 proposes it. It is the first interface of the daemon that is not a file.
- **Whether the daemon uploads for a pool nobody vouched for.** A cut is a complete pool only if the volume has held the whole pool since its creation (design §5.2, the premise; from the review of M2.1). The agent checks the layout before a publish, but the daemon uploads recovery points on its own. Whether an upload waits for the agent's word that the pool is one plain data device on this volume is decided with M2.5's channel.
- **How large the mapped state is.** It is locked in memory (design §5.2), so its size for each TiB of volume is RAM the daemon holds. M2.2's first proposal, a table of about 9 bytes for each block (2.3 GB for a TiB at 4 KiB), is too large to lock; the lead asked on 2026-11-14 for a representation in the order of 100 MiB for a TiB. Coding's second proposal, which M2.2a builds: four maps of one bit a block (the cut being uploaded, the one that waits, the one whose flush is under way, the open map), each with a summary bit for every 64-bit word so that a pass touches only what is set, a header, and a record of 16 bytes for every copy (the block, and the epochs from and to which the copy is its state; the copy's place in the copies file is the record's index). At 4 KiB blocks that is 130 MiB for a TiB of volume plus a 256th of the bound, all locked. A freeze is one store (the count of cuts and which map is open share a word). Taking a map is one store that names the taken map as a second part of the heir's, a pass that sets each word in the larger part before it clears it in the smaller, and one store that names the larger alone; a death in the pass leaves the union whole and the next start finishes it.
- **The bound's provisional default** is a 256th of the volume and at least 256 MiB (4 GiB for a TiB, 16 MiB of records), in place of the eighth first written here, because the records are locked.
- **Discards are not noted in M2** (design §5.2, §5.3, amended): a hole needs a fifth bit a block, or a copy before the discard, and holes in cuts are M4's discard tracking. M2.T3's comparison with the reference takes either content for a block discarded since it was written.
- **What the mapped state's sync is for.** The state is trusted only in the boot that wrote it, so a sync of it buys nothing across a reboot today. Whether an orderly stop should leave a state the next boot may trust is a question for M2.2 to put to the lead with what it would cost and save.
- **A copy that is lost with no trace** (review of M2.2b-1, Q1, and Codex on skyzfs/skyzfs#100). The copies are written buffered. A writeback error of the copies file is lost if its inode is evicted while no daemon holds the file, so the next start's sync succeeds over a copy that never reached the disk; and a volume can fail with neither marker nor failure note written. In both the next start takes up a held cut one of whose copies is gone. Decided by the lead 2026-11-15, to be built as M2.2c before M2.4: the copies are written by direct I/O, as the image is, so a copy that cannot be written fails at its write and lets the cuts go then. M2.T4 measures what that costs the write path; a checksum for each copy in the state is the fallback if the cost is too high. Built and merged (skyzfs/skyzfs#104): the copies are written and read by direct I/O when the store's I/O is direct. Its cost on the write path is M2.T4's to measure, and the kernel has not yet driven it with cuts held: the cut rig's content stage (skyzfs/skyzfs#103) does that. Still open from its review: nothing says why a copy was refused (a copies file without direct I/O shows only as a climbing count of abandoned cuts); the reason goes into the status with M2.4.
- **A refused start and the start token** (review of M2.2b-1, B1). Every open writes the token, a start that is then refused included (seen for a start refused for `data=journal`). The cuts held before a refused start are let go, and after M2.4 the next cut counts the whole volume. The lead first decided the other way, the token after the refusals that change nothing, and took it back the same day on the author's and the review's advice: that way rests on "nothing was changed yet", the reasoning that M2.2b-1's two trust findings got wrong, and a mistake there uploads a cut that is not the volume, while this way costs one upload of the whole volume after a refusal that needs an operator anyway. The daemon rig's pin is widened to match: a refused start may change the status file and `volume.start`, nothing else.
- **M2.4c as built** (skyzfs/skyzfs#117, #118; the facts for operators are in `CLAUDE.md`, "The daemon uploads"). The volume's identity lives in the file `remote` written by `create`, never in a serve option, so a serve given the wrong name cannot put a whole cut on another volume's head. The uploader is a one-thread runtime `skyzfsd-upload`; each `Local` call goes to the queue's thread through the desk, answered after each turn. Status version 15 is 976 bytes. Open, listed on purpose (F5, a renewal that was a read and then a take, is fixed: see "F5 as built" below): a volume created without a place cannot be given one later (M3's restore flow); `create --replace` for a half-made create; a clone that stopped before its head (review 2.3); `--intent-region-mib` still needs `--hold-cuts`; `cuts_replaced` is in memory and not kept across a start; the desk waits for the copies file's sync on the queue's thread (fixed in #118 for the copies' sync, the figure to measure with M2.2e and Q7: whether the intent map's header is synced at each release, to be decided with the bench on this build); the head is matched by group alone (after a rewind import two commits can share a group); M4's garbage collection must spare segments an upload under way has put early, and collects those a given-up upload leaves. Decided: the first cut after an acknowledged failure is a delta onto the map's base when the start took the map up (rule 2), whole only when the map was begun anew or after a mismatch (rule 3).
- **What the header sync costs, with a real uploader (M2.T4 round 4, run 44358962767 at 405b7ed, 2026-11-18; testing's figures).** One job on ubuntu-26.04, all seven rows on one runner, origin delay 0, a 32 GiB volume holding about 1 GiB of pool, uploads to MinIO on the runner; sequence m1, off1, on1, m2, on2, off2, m3 (off is `--hold-cuts`, on adds `--intent-header-sync`), release build. The seed (the first whole cut, the pool imported, synced and idle) committed 178, 178, 179 and 179 s after the start in the four upload rows, with no cut lost. tps and the ratio to the bracketing model rows (model rows 3665, 3643, 3775 in tpcb; 30760, 30662, 31066 in select): tpcb off1 3146 (0.861), on1 3183 (0.871), on2 3118 (0.841), off2 3120 (0.841); select off1 25649 (0.835), on1 25501 (0.830), on2 25642 (0.831), off2 25278 (0.819); tpcb mean latency 2.51 to 2.56 ms against 2.11 to 2.19 for the model. On against off, two rows each: tpcb 3150 against 3133 (+0.6%), select 25572 against 25464 (+0.4%), both inside the model rows' spread (3.6% in tpcb). Every ratio is below 0.90, as with `--hold-cuts` and no upload in round 3. The uploader in all eight windows: 24 to 25 uploads, all deltas, none lost, replaced or abandoned, no mismatch or error, 562k to 598k blocks in a tpcb window and 120k to 124k in select. The intent map per window (mean of two rows): map syncs 692 (off) and 695 (on) in tpcb, 271 and 273 in select; 0.55 and 0.64 ms per map sync in tpcb, 0.48 and 0.49 in select; the longest map sync 35 to 45 ms; header syncs 25 in tpcb and 24 to 25 in select, 0.08 to 0.11 ms each, longest 0.77 to 0.97 ms; writes that waited for a map sync 805 to 825 (off) and 819 to 820 (on) in tpcb, 347 to 363 in select; regions named at a window's end at most 19 of 8192. The bucket after each upload row: 5075 to 5118 MiB in 1587 to 1591 objects, no unfinished upload. **Decided (Q7, lead):** the header sync costs nothing that shows, so it is on by default and the option becomes `--no-intent-header-sync`, since it saves a mismatch (and a whole upload) after a power cut that lost the header's last write (Q8). No perf row yet (M2.2e): the pair after M2.4e carries one. The leak of the first run (about 72 GiB over four rows) is not confirmed or ruled out by these rows, since nothing was lost in them.
- **The first upload under a write load with M2.4e's holes, and a profile (M2.T4 round 5, run 44372469282, daemon = #125's head 0f56669, 2026-11-18; testing's figures).** One job on ubuntu-26.04 x86_64, one boot id, 60 s windows, tpcb and select, 32 GiB volume with about 1 GiB of pool, uploads to MinIO; sequence m1, on1, off1, m2, idle, perf, m3; every daemon row `--hold-cuts` with uploads, all but off1 with `--intent-header-sync`; idle seeds with the pool idle, the others start the workload during the seed, perf adds `perf record -g`. *The seed (corrected, 2026-11-18, after review's F5 on skyzfs/skyzfs#123):* the rows with the wait at 0 s (on1, off1, perf) are NOT shown to have seeded under the load: the seed committed 16 to 17 s before pgbench's warm-up began, so the label "after N s under the workload" was false and the seed was not tested against a load by this run. The first upload committed 22 s (on1), 22 s (off1) and 21 s (perf) after the start and 16 s idle, with no cut lost or abandoned; before M2.4e it was 178 to 179 s idle (round 4, another runner) and never under load (run 44325181328). What shows the holes working under a write load is the cut rig (run 44378510338: 320 MiB written into holes under a held whole cut, nothing abandoned; the daemon copied about 0.75 to 1 MiB during the hold). A bench row with the load running before and during the seed is still to be made. At each window's start `upload_zero_blocks` is 8128223 to 8128732 (about 31 GiB of the 32 GiB written as zero extents with nothing read), `uploaded_whole` 1, `cut_copied_bytes` 0, `cuts_abandoned` 0, and `cut_holes` reads `dropped` with `cut_hole_chunks` 524288 at both ends of every window (a question for coding: whether `dropped` with the full chunk count is as intended). *The windows:* 23 to 25 uploads per window, all deltas, none lost, replaced, abandoned or mismatched, no error, lease held at the end, 768k to 798k blocks in tpcb and 121k to 130k in select. *The bucket after each upload row:* on1 6231 MiB (1651 objects), off1 6248 (1691), idle 6230 (1694), perf 6356 (1703), no unfinished upload, work disk about 97 GiB free; no cut was lost, so this round does not test the suspected leak of unreferenced segments. *tps and the ratio to the model:* tpcb (m1 5733, m2 5472, m3 6065) on1 4754 (0.848), off1 4699 (0.839), idle 4658 (0.807, no verdict, bracket 10.3%), perf 4939 (recorded); select (m1 48901, m2 50297, m3 50274) on1 41059 (0.828), off1 41549 (0.838), idle 41358 (0.822), perf 37650 (recorded). Every verdict is BELOW 0.90, as in rounds 3 and 4. The windows of idle, on1 and off1 are within 1 to 2% of each other (not a comparison of seeding under load, see the correction above). *The header-sync pair* on1 against off1: tpcb +1.2%, select -1.2%, inside the model rows' spread (4.7% and 2.8%); header syncs 23 to 25 per window at 0.07 to 0.10 ms, longest 0.89 ms; map syncs 857 to 886 per tpcb window at 0.42 to 0.52 ms and 245 to 269 per select window at 0.37 to 0.52 ms; regions named at the end 0 to 22 of 8192 (Q7 stands as decided). *The profile (M2.2e, `perf record -g`):* the queue thread is 77% kernel in select and 69% in tpcb, of the kernel samples wake and switch 41%, ZFS completion 19% and 14%, io_uring 18% and 19%, the ublk driver 12% and 9%, copy in ublk 4% and 9%; `skyzfs-io` is 90 to 91% kernel, of it wake and switch 59% and 53%, page cache 35% and 42%; `skyzfsd-upload` has 130 (select) and 618 (tpcb) samples against 1990 to 4192 for the queue thread, 18 to 21% kernel, mostly copies, and used 0.7 s (select) and about 3.8 s (tpcb) of CPU per window against 24 s and 11 s for the queue thread. The uploader is a small share of the daemon's CPU; the gap to the model is in the request path (wake and switch, the page cache under `skyzfs-io`), which is not M2's to close and is for M3's tuning.
- **The seed under a real load, on a volume with holes and on one without (M2.T5, run 44412222348, daemon 804f65d = #125's head with status v17, 2026-11-18; testing's figures, skyzfs/skyzfs#128).** One job, one machine for all rows (boot 815979b0, ubuntu-26.04), one run per row, tpcb then select, 120 s, 8 clients, 1 GiB memory limit, origin delay 0, daemon `--hold-cuts --intent-header-sync`, 32 GiB volume; rows m1, zf, m2, fl, sp, m3. Model (zfs-slog) tps: tpcb 7578, 7891, 7754; select 54918, 57542, 56032. **zf** (`create --zero-fill`, wait 0 s, so the seed under load): tpcb 5424 (0.701, bracket 4.0%), select 39617 (0.705, 4.7%), NO VERDICT because no upload committed. No upload landed: 19 and 2 whole cuts abandoned and lost in the two windows, 33 by the end of select; copies reached the 256 MiB bound (at the windows' ends `cut_copied_bytes` was 161210368 after tpcb and 128192512 after select, that is 153.7 and 122.3 MiB; at their starts 15.8 and 129.3 MiB; corrected after review's R1, the first version read MB as MiB); `cut_holes` held with 0 chunks; the first upload was still NONE 356 s after the import. After the row MinIO held 22702 MiB in 2835 objects (about 0.7 GiB per abandoned attempt, inferred), with no commit. The uploader used 11.6 to 11.7 s of user CPU per window; work disk 35.3 KiB per transaction; txg sync mean 1414 ms. **fl** (sparse, `TB_SKZ_UPLOAD_FROM_LOAD=1`, MinIO frozen until the workload began): tpcb 6509 (0.832, 1.8%), select 48564 (0.855, 2.7%), BELOW 0.90; the seed committed 52 s after the import, under the workload, 11 to 16 s after its warm-up began, with 524288 chunks (all of the 32 GiB) as holes and 8127449 zero blocks sent with nothing read; 24 and 23 uploads, none abandoned; MinIO 7767 MiB. **sp** (sparse, wait 0 s): tpcb 6589 (0.842, 1.8%), select 46603 (0.821, 2.7%), BELOW 0.90; the seed committed 15 s after the import, before the workload; 25 and 23 uploads, none abandoned; MinIO 8243 MiB. In the sparse rows the uploader used 4.7 to 4.8 s of user CPU in a tpcb window and under 1 s in select; work disk 19.7 to 19.9 KiB per transaction; txg sync mean 452 to 480 ms. So M2.4e does what it was built for on a sparse volume: a seed that starts under a real load lands with no copy (52 s after the import, of which 36 s MinIO was frozen by the row's design: the seed itself took 11 to 16 s from MinIO's answer, review's R2), and the windows after it are as in the other sparse rows (`fl`'s windows hold no part of the seed: `uploaded_whole` was already 1 at the tpcb window's start and the seed ended within the 30 s warm-up, so its ratios are figures from after the seed, like `sp`'s; review's R3). **What it does not do** (a volume with no holes, or a sparse one with real overwrites during the seed): the first whole cut is abandoned at the bound again and again, nothing lands while the load runs, and each attempt leaves about 0.7 GiB of unreferenced segments in the bucket, here 22.7 GiB in 356 s (about 64 MiB/s) until M4's garbage collection; the volume has no recovery point in object storage for as long as the load lasts. Decided (lead): the garbage's growth is bounded now (a backoff after an abandoned whole cut, and a delete of the segments of the attempt itself if that is safe: coding to propose), the idle seed time of a zero-filled volume is measured next (a row with a wait over 0), and a seed that can never land under load is M3's to answer (the restore and attach flow, a quiesced seed).
- **M2.4e as built** (skyzfs/skyzfs#125; the figures that led to it are in the entry on the first upload under a write load). The cut state is version 3 (`state::VERSION`; version 2 is still read and a version 2 state is kept at its own length until a begin in a region of version 3's length), with a hole trailer: a word for the set's state, one for its count of chunks, and a bit for each 64 KiB chunk of the image that is a hole (SEEK_HOLE). The set is taken only at a begin that makes the whole volume count as changed, never refreshed, and written before the state's magic, so a death inside a begin leaves no state or the whole set (tested at every store); the set is persisted with the state, so a restart or a take-over before the first commit keeps it. Until the state's first cut is committed and let go, a block in such a chunk that no write has marked holds zeros, known without a read: a whole cut makes no copy and no record for a write into it (the bit for "written since the begin" is set before the write is acknowledged), and reads a run of such blocks as one zero extent of any length in one turn, looking for the run's end 4096 blocks at a time (the first version was near-quadratic, about 2 minutes on the queue thread at 1 TiB; the F4 test bounds the loads by a multiple of one pass and by the loads of 16 GiB). A chunk or block is a hole only whole. Letting the first cut go drops the set. A start where SEEK_DATA is unsupported or answers wrongly has no set and behaves as before. `cut_holes` reads `dropped` after the first commit with `cut_hole_chunks` as recorded at the begin (kept on purpose, documented). Status version 17 (1032 bytes). Open on purpose: a volume with no holes, or real overwrites during the seed, still copy every block written under the first cut (see the entry on the seed under a real load). Kernel evidence: the cut rig's `bound` on a zero-filled volume abandons its cut at the bound, `boundsparse` on a sparse image writes 320 MiB into holes with 0 abandoned (the copies figure is read after the let-go and not judged, review's N3), `held_content` compares 393216 blocks on amd64 and 262144 on arm64 with no difference; and run 44412222348's sparse rows.
- **The `--zero-fill` seed under load with M2.4f's backoff (run 44439485799, daemon b75a128 = an earlier head of M2.4f, skyzfs/skyzfs#129, merged at 83899d2; testing's figures, 2026-11-19).** One job on one machine (ubuntu-26.04, kernel 7.0.0-1020-azure), sequence m1 zfs-slog, zf, m2 zfs-slog, one run per row of 120 s for tpcb and one for select, origin delay 0, `--hold-cuts --intent-header-sync`, upload on with wait 0, the volume made with `--zero-fill` (written in 83.6 s). tps: tpcb zf 3018 against m1 3585 and m2 3547 (0.846, bracket 1.1%); select 17693 against 22905 and 22799 (0.774, 0.5%); both NO VERDICT because no upload committed. Uploads 0 committed, the first upload still NONE 352 s after the import. v19 fields read at the start of tpcb, the end of tpcb and the end of select: `upload_state` whole_lost each time, `upload_cuts_lost_whole` 1, 2, 3 (the first was lost in the warm-up), `upload_wait_ms` 60000, 120000, 240000 (doubling as specified), `upload_why` "whole_cut_N went before its commit: no such held cut". Cuts abandoned 2 then 12 over tpcb, 16 then 17 over select; replaced 21 then 35, then 39 then 62. MinIO at the end: 2568 MiB in 324 objects, no unfinished multipart upload, against 22702 MiB in 2835 objects and 33 whole cuts lost on 804f65d (run 44412222348): 8.8 times fewer MiB and 8.7 times fewer objects (single runs on different machines; the byte count does not depend on the machine's speed as tps does, and the tps ratios cannot be compared with that run's 0.701 and 0.705). So the backoff bounds the leak and a zero-filled volume under this load still never seeds, as decided; the idle seed time of such a volume (wait over 0) is testing's next measurement. Segments that attempts leave behind are M4's garbage collection (the delete of an attempt's own segments, option ii, is not built).
- **The idle seed of a zero-filled volume, and a seed's cost inside a window (M2.T5b, run 44462902930, daemon 861b537; testing's figures, 2026-11-19, skyzfs/skyzfs#132).** One sequence job, six rows on one machine (ubuntu-26.04 x86_64, kernel 7.0.0-1019-azure), one run per row, tpcb then select, origin delay 0, `--hold-cuts --intent-header-sync`; bracket widths 0.1 to 1.1%. Model rows (zfs-slog) tpcb 3581, 3566, 3584, select 23011, 23035, 23286. **zi** (`--zero-fill`, the seed on an idle pool, wait 1800 s): the seed committed 227 s after the import; tpcb 0.895 (BELOW 0.90), select 0.846; no zero blocks sent (holes `dropped (0)`), daemon RSS 244 MiB (216 in sp), no reclaim, no cgroup limit hit. **sw** (sparse, the seed released at the first window's start): committed 10 to 15 s after MinIO was let go, inside the tpcb window; tpcb 0.870 (NO VERDICT, see below), select 0.839; in that window 969410 blocks uploaded (571214 in sp), 8127964 blocks written as zeros with nothing read (0 in sp), the upload thread 5.6 s user and 1.7 s system (3.3 and 1.0 in sp), work disk 27.8 KiB per transaction (22.9), tps 3110 against sp's 3224. **sp** (sparse, the seed at the workload's start): committed 16 s after the import, before the workload; tpcb 0.902, select 0.858. So a zero-filled volume seeds on an idle pool in under four minutes (227 s for 32 GiB, no hole shortcut), a sparse volume's seed inside a write window costs about 3.5% of tps in that window and about 2.3 more seconds of upload thread CPU, and every row but one is below 0.90 as in earlier rounds. A rig finding: freezing MinIO until the window begins also holds back the lease, so a window-mode row starts with no lease (epoch 0, then epoch 1) and `bracket.not_uploading` reads NO VERDICT.
- **Q7 as built: the intent map's header is synced by default** (skyzfs/skyzfs#133, merged at e40fb43; the figures are in "M2.T4 round 4"). `serve` syncs the header at each release unless `--no-intent-header-sync` is given, which is taken only with `--hold-cuts`; `--intent-header-sync` is gone and a start given it exits 2. A volume that uploads holds its cuts without `--hold-cuts` and syncs the header too. The status's `intent_header_sync` reads 1 where cuts are held unless the option was given. The sync is kept on a failed volume (F-A of #124's review: a release that reaches a store that has already failed still begins a sync, so the next start, which acknowledges the failure, finds a header that decides whether the next cut is a delta), and two tests (n1, n3) pin that the drop does not wait for it. **Benchmark comparisons:** rows with `--hold-cuts` before e40fb43 ran with the header sync off and after it with it on, so rows with the same label on either side are not the same configuration (round 4 measured the pair: +0.6% tpcb and +0.4% select, inside the spread). Open, low: one deadline for a client's request to the channel (S4 of the review of #131; the client waits up to about three limits against a daemon that stalls in each phase).
- **F1 as built: the refs row names the commit it was written for** (skyzfs/skyzfs#134, merged at 2c7e4bb). Row v2 is v1's 34 bytes plus the head's commit hash (66 bytes), judged and read by commit. A v1 row names its cut and no commit, so the first commit after an upgrade is whole and writes v2. A downgrade to a daemon from before F1 stops uploads and is unsupported (N2). Publishes keep waiting for head to match (N6). A `row_v1` reason (status code 6) is given only where the v1 row names the cut's base and head's group is the row's; another group stays `head_moved`, another cut `not_the_base` (N3). Node B's reader reads v2. A new status code that an older reader takes as "not said" may be added with no version bump (CONTRIBUTING.md; precedent 08cfe8d). Review's B1 was fixed and tested. Rigs at 7989f5e: daemon rig 56 PASS on 24.04, 26.04 and 26.04-arm; cut rig on the daemon 15 of 15 on both 26.04 images and the upload job 6 of 6.
- **M2.T7 as built: the publish rig** (skyzfs/skyzfs#139, merged at 6758025). Run 45539186244 (ubuntu-26.04, one run, one runner) at c7cc03f, every line ok. `pplain`: t1 by cut 10 of group 18 (C 8, G 17); t4 asked with G 43 above createtxg 23 passed over 8 cuts of groups 23 to 42 and was made by cut 23 of group 43, so no cut below G was what `snap/t4` named. `prewind` (at c7cc03f): 2 cuts held at the rewind (C 20, G 31); node B's row named cut 22 of group 32, which opens only as a rewind; r1 was made later by cut 27 of group 45, which opens as itself. `pkill`: the instance that answered tw's withdrawal counted 4 cuts of group 43 or later that open as itself and committed cut 19 of group 43; tw has no ref and no row after the kill. `pkillman` and `pkillpub`: killed by the hook; p1 found on the commit that landed before the kill. `pchannel`: control_answered 4, control_peer 1, control_malformed 2, socket 0600. Review's B2 to B4 (two courses that passed without testing their point, the expected table not pinned) were fixed. The deferred W5 to W7 and the watcher's VERDICT-lines gap go into the bench and test-gap package.
- **M2.T7b as built: the publish rig's `look` and `-F` courses** (skyzfs/skyzfs#141, merged at c7f9613). Run 45569275289 (ubuntu-26.04, one run) at b76ab97, seven courses ok. `prewindf`: group 34 zeroed, `-F` refused, the pool imported with `-FX` (as `rewindf` does); cut 17 was of group 34, equal to the top; the request log ends f1 `refused rewound` 44 ms after that cut and f1 is absent. `prewind`: ground cuts 21 and 22 (groups 31 and 32), top 43; r1-look read `refused store` with the store down. Premises read the request log, the agent's records or the pool, never the daemon's own answers, so a real fault is a verdict (exit 1) and not the rig's error (exit 2). `prewindf` pins only that the mark ends a waiting publish; "opens as itself" with a publish waiting is exercised by the unit tests and the cut rig. The prewind and prewindf lines are a verdict on rule (R) at the ask. The daemon and replay rigs were cancelled at the last two heads, which change only `cut-record.sh` and the publish files; their green stands at e0c51f8 (runs 45562018042 and 45562018051). Review's B1 and B2 were fixed; N3, N4 and S1 (a no-rewind control) are non-blocking.
- **M2.T5e as built: stage 0 of the agent rig** (skyzfs/skyzfs#145, merged at 8cb0e80). The kernel probe now pins what the agent depends on. On an idle pool a bare `zpool sync` writes no group (0 on both images); a `zfs set` of a new value then `zpool sync` writes one (24.04: groups 90, 97 after the set, 98 after the sync; 26.04: 91, 98, 99); 30 assists at 1/s wrote 60 groups and left 60 empty, at about 320 to 327 kB a group, so the assist costs two groups through the CLI. `zinject -e io -T flush` is refused on ZFS 2.2 (24.04) and works on 26.04 (an INFO line). Daemon loss with reissue is invisible to ZFS, 5 of 5 kills on each image (probe run 45650635318, kernels 7.0.0-1017 and 7.0.0-1020). The publish rig gained `passist` and `prewindc` and the F1 and G3/G4 checks; `passist` a2 ran 8 assists in 52.9 s, 16 groups that wrote and 19 empty, 16 cuts counted and 19 cuts (688 blocks, about 36 a cut) uploaded in 70.2 s, and a1 was carried by cut 12 of group 22, about 11 s after acceptance, in each of four runs. The floors of the assist's pace are verdicts and the ceilings are the INFO line `obs_assist_gap_over_3x`; `pidle` was made monotonic (flaky before, clean over 2000 runs). The cut rig retries a busy export (`obs_export_refusals` reads 0 in all 10 recordings on four images). Merged on review's verdict and green CI; the daemon and replay rigs were cancelled and not run, since nothing they run changed. For stage 1: S5 (the burst floor grows with A while the agent pauses a fixed 1 s), T3 to T5, N1, N2.
- **M2.T5d as built: the bench and test-gap package** (skyzfs/skyzfs#143, merged at 6b9d4a0). S19, S19b, W5 to W7, N9, N10, N18 to N21, O7, the seed rule's clause, `upload_found_committed` against 0, the watcher's VERDICT lines, and #141's N3, N4 and S1 (the control course `prewindc`: a plain import with no ground of rule (R); c1 waited 60 s across 56 cuts in its first kernel run). Publish rig green on all eight courses, cut rig 10 of 10 on four images, cut rig on the daemon 15 of 15 and upload 8 of 8. Codex did not review (usage limit); merged on review's verdict and green CI, the package being rig and test code only. The daemon and replay rigs were cancelled by the author, since nothing they run changed. Not taken: #141's S2, S3 and its plant survivors (listed in the PR).
- **M2.6a as built: the daemon's side of publish** (skyzfs/skyzfs#140, merged at 5447677). Rule (R) is a running maximum of the groups the store has seen since its start (rings included); a cut not above it, or a publish whose count is below it or below the count the daemon started with, is refused `rewound`; the live mark is read with each `publish`. New verb `look` (a name's row: absent, `snap`, unreadable, `store`, `no-answer`). The protocol moved to `crates/skyzfs-control` and is version 2 (a v1 client rejects the new words as a parse error and fails closed; none is deployed). A rewind to a checkpoint is not marked: the agent refuses a pool with a checkpoint (design §6.2). Rig: daemon 56 of 56 on three images and 33 on the stand-in twice, cut rig 10 of 10 on four images, cut rig on the daemon 15 of 15 and upload 8 of 8, publish rig green with the courses changed to assert `refused rewound` and then ask anew (pkillpub's p1 ends `refused taken`). Plants: 20 re-run at the final head, 18 caught; Wb and Q5 survive and get tests in M2.6b1. Cost accepted: after `-T` each cut up to the abandoned top moves the mark, and a pool made anew on the same daemon over-marks; both only delay publishes.
- **M2.6b1 as built: the agent crate** (skyzfs/skyzfs#142, merged at 06b8f8e). `crates/skyzfs-agent` (library, blocking, injected clock): names (`skz-<UTC>`, no mapping of `:`), the state record (SZAG v1, postcard, BLAKE3, written tmp-fsync-rename-directory fsync; corrupt means publish nothing and report; golden bytes pin it), the manifest (canonical JSON v1, at most 1 MiB), and the planner as a pure function with a table row per phase and per daemon answer. `snap/<t>` counts as ours when its row records our manifest hash and a cut at or after the snapshot's group; the request's count is not compared, because the daemon applied rule (R) to whichever request landed. The assist is bounded: one sync once ASSIST_AFTER (10 s) has passed since the last, from twice ASSIST_AFTER after `accepted`.
